<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{def:paramMonads}
	For categories $\mathbb{C}$ and $\mathbb{D}$, a \emph{parametrized monad} consists of a functor $T : \mathbb{C}^\mathrm{op} \times \mathbb{C} \times \mathbb{D} \to \mathbb{D}$ together with 
	\begin{itemize}
		\item a family of morphisms $\eta_{S,X}: X \to T(S,S,X)$ indexed by objects $S$ in $\mathbb{C}$ and $X$ in $\mathbb{D}$ natural in $X$ and dinatural in $S$
		\item a family of morphisms $\mu_{S_1 ,S_2 ,S_3 ,X}	: T(S_1,S_2,T(S_2,S_3,X))\to T(S_1,S_3,X)$ indexed by objects $S_1,S_2,S_3$ in $\mathbb{C}$ and $X$ in $\mathbb{D}$ natural in $X, S_1,S_3$ and dinatural in $S_2$
	\end{itemize}
	satisfying some compatibility axioms.
	If $(\mathbb{D}, \otimes, 1)$ is monoidal, a \emph{parametrized strong monad} is additionally equipped with 
	\begin{itemize}
 		\item a family of morphisms $\mathrm{str}_{S_1 ,S_2 ,X,Y}	: X \otimes T(S_1, S_2,Y) \to  T(S_1,S_2,X \otimes Y)$ in $\mathbb{D}$ indexed by objects $S_1,S_2$ in $\mathbb{C}$ and $X,Y$ in $\mathbb{D}$, natural in all variables, and satisfying some compatibility axioms.
	\end{itemize}
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- example-1: body_md -->
\label{ex:paramStateMonad}
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item The parametrized state monad~\cite[Section 2.3.2]{atkey2009parameterised}: for a fixed set of states $S$, the global state monad maps a set $X$ to the set $(X \times S)^S$ while the parametrized version allows for the set of input states and output states to vary, its underlying functor $T : \mathbf{Set}^\mathrm{op} \times \mathbf{Set} \times \mathbf{Set} \to \mathbf{Set}$ has the following action on objects: $(S_1, S_2, A) \mapsto (A \times S_2)^{S_1}$.
		\item The composable continuation monad \cite[Example 5.2.6]{atkey2006substructural}:
		for a fixed response object $R$ in a cartesian closed category, the ordinary continuation monad maps an object $X$ to $(X \Rightarrow R) \Rightarrow R$. In the parametrized case, we obtain the notion of composable continuations where the response type may vary: the composable continuation monad $T : \mathbf{Set}^\mathrm{op} \times \mathbf{Set} \times \mathbf{Set} \to \mathbf{Set}$ is given by $(R_1, R_2, X) \mapsto 
		(X \Rightarrow R_1)\Rightarrow R_2$.
		\item The parametrized I/O monad~\cite[Section 2.3.4]{atkey2009parameterised}: for a fixed set of inputs $I$ and observable outputs $O$, the interactive input-output (I/O) monad  is the functor mapping a set $X$ to the underlying set of the initial algebra $ \mu Z. (X + Z^I+ O\times Z)$. It induces three possible types of computations: either compute a value, wait for an input and then resume or produce an output and then resume~\cite[Example 1.1]{moggi1991notions}. 
		The parametrized $I/O$-monad $\mathbf{Set}^\mathrm{op} \times \mathbf{Set} \times \mathbf{Set} \to \mathbf{Set}$ maps a triple $(I, O, X)$ to the underlying set of $\mu Z. (X + Z^I + O\times Z)$.
		\item The parametrized action monad \cite[Example 5.2.5]{atkey2006substructural}: any monoid $(M, e: I \to M, m : M \otimes M \to M)$ internal to a monoidal category $\mathbb{C}$ induces a monad (called the writer, output or action monad) on $\mathbb{C}$ given by $X \mapsto M \otimes X$. This construction has been generalized by Atkey in the case of parametrized monads where any category $\mathbb{P}$ induces a parametrized monad $\mathbb{P}^\mathrm{op} \times \mathbb{P} \times \mathbf{Set} \to \mathbf{Set}$ whose action on objects is $(A,B, X) \mapsto \mathbb{P}(A,B) \times X$.
	\end{enumerate}
<!-- end example-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{def:strictParamExtension}
	For a functor $T : \mathbf{Set}^\mathrm{op} \times \mathbf{Set} \times \mathbf{Set} \to \mathbf{Set}$, a $1$-functor $\overline{T} :  \mathbf{Rel} \times \mathbf{Rel} \times \mathbf{Rel} \to \mathbf{Rel}$ extends $T$ strictly if the following holds:
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item for all $f : A \to B, g : C\to D$ and $h : X\to Y$, $\overline{T}(f^*,g_*,h_*)=(T(f,g,h))_*$
\item we say that $\overline{T}$ is \emph{monotone} if for all $M'\leq M : A  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B$, $N\leq N': C  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 D$ and $R\leq R' : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$, $\overline{T}(M ,N, R) \leq \overline{T}(M' ,N',R')$.
	\end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
\label{def:laxextension}
	For a functor $F: \mathbf{Set} \to \mathbf{Set}$, a \emph{lax extension} of $F$ to $\mathbf{Rel}$ consists of an indexed family of functions $\overline{T}:\mathbf{Rel}(X,Y) \longrightarrow \mathbf{Rel}(TX, TY)$ satisfying the following axioms:
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item $\overline{T}$ is monotone: if $R \leq R' : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$, then $\overline{T}(R) \leq \overline{T}(R')$;
		\item for composable relations $R : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$, $S : Y  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Z$, $\overline{T}(S) \circ \overline{T}(R) \leq \overline{T}(S \circ R)$;
		\item for all functions $f: X \to Y$;
		$(T(f))_* \leq \overline{T}(f_*)$ and $(T(f))^* \leq \overline{T}(f^*)$.
	\end{enumerate}
	If $\overline{T}$ preserves identities strictly, \emph{i.e.}\; $\mathrm{id}_{TX} = \overline{T}(\mathrm{id}_X)$, we say that it is a \emph{normal} lax extension (also called \emph{flat}). If conditions $2.$ and $3.$ are equalities, then we recover exactly the notion of monotone strict extension.
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- example-2: body_md -->
In the case of the power set functor $\mathcal{P}: \mathbf{Set} \to \mathbf{Set}$, for a relation $R : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$, the following mappings
	\[\begin{aligned}
		\mathcal{L}_1(R)&:=\{ (A,B) \in \mathcal{P}(X)\times \mathcal{P}(Y) \mid \forall x \in A, \exists y \in B, (x,y) \in R\}\\
		\mathcal{L}_2(R)&:=\{ (A,B) \in \mathcal{P}(X)\times \mathcal{P}(Y) \mid \forall y \in B, \exists x \in A, (x,y) \in R\}\\
		\mathcal{L}_3(R)&:=	\mathcal{L}_1(R) \cap 	\mathcal{L}_2(R)
	\end{aligned}\]
	are all lax extensions. The last one corresponds to the canonical Barr extension in Section~\ref{subsec:ordinaryBarr}.
<!-- end example-2 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
\label{def:ParamLaxExtensionMonadSet}
	For a functor $T: \mathbf{Set}^\mathrm{op} \times \mathbf{Set} \times \mathbf{Set} \to \mathbf{Set}$, a \emph{parametrized lax extension} consists of a family of functions: $\overline{T}:	\mathbf{Rel}(A,B) \times \mathbf{Rel}(C,D) \times \mathbf{Rel}(X, Y)  \to \mathbf{Rel}(T(A, C,X), T(B,D,Y))$
	indexed by $A,B,C,D,X,Y$ that satisfies the following axioms:
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item it is antitone in the first argument and monotone in the last two arguments:  for all $M'\leq M : A  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B$, $N\leq N': C  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 D$ and $R\leq R' : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$, 
		\[\overline{T}(M ,N, R) \leq \overline{T}(M' ,N',R')\]
		\item lax composition: for all $M: A_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 A_2$, $M' :A_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 A_3$,  $N: B_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_2$, $N' :B_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_3$, $R: X_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 X_2$ and $R' :X_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 X_3$, 
		\[\overline{T}(M,N,R)  ; \overline{T}(M',N',R') \leq  	\overline{T}(M; M',N; N',R; R')\]
		\item for all $f : A\to B$, $g : C \to D$ and $h : X \to Y$, 
		\[(T(f, g, h))_* \leq \overline{T}(f^*, g_*, h_* )\text{ and }(T(f, g, h))^*\leq \overline{T}(f_*,  g^*, h^* ).\]
	\end{enumerate}
		If $\overline{T}$ preserves identities strictly $(\mathrm{id}_{T(A,B,X)} = \overline{T}(\mathrm{id}_{A}, \mathrm{id}_{B},\mathrm{id}_X))$, we say that it is a \emph{normal} parametrized lax extension. If conditions $2.$ and $3.$ are equalities, then we obtain a monotone strict extension.
		If $\overline{T}$ commutes with the transpose operation as below, it is called \emph{symmetric}:
		\begin{enumerate}
			    \setcounter{enumi}{3}
			\item for all $M : A  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B$, $N: C  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 D$ and $R : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$, $(\overline{T}(M ,N, R))^t = \overline{T}(M^t ,N^t,R^t)$
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- example-3: body_md -->
\label{ex:statenostrictextension}
The underlying functor of the parametrized state monad (Example~\ref{ex:paramStateMonad}.1) has a parametrized lax extension $\overline{T}$ which we describe explicitly: for relations $M : A  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B$, $N: C  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 D$ and $R : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$, $\overline{T}(M,N,R): (C \times X)^A  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 (D \times Y)^B$ is given by:
\[
(N \times R)^M:=\{ (\varphi, \psi) \mid \forall(a,b) \in M, (\varphi(a), \psi(b)) \in N \times R\}.
\]
<!-- end example-3 -->
<!-- definition-5: body_md -->
\label{def:weakpb}
	A commutative square $h\circ f= k \circ g$ in $\mathbf{Set}$ as below is a weak pullback if the universal map from $A$ to the pullback of $h$ and $k$ is split epi.
	\vspace{-0.1cm}
	\begin{center}
		\begin{tikzpicture}[thick,xscale=0.5, yscale=0.6]
			\node (A) at (0,1.5) {\scriptsize$B$};
			\node (B) at (2,0) {\scriptsize$D$};
			\node (C) at (4,1.5) {\scriptsize$C$};
			\node (D) at (2,3.5) {\scriptsize$A$};
			\node (P) at (2,2.25) {\scriptsize$P$};
			\draw [-to, dotted] (D)  to  node [below left] {} (P);
			\draw [->] (A)  to  node [below left] {\scriptsize$h$} (B);
			\draw [->] (C)  to  node [below right] {\scriptsize$k$} (B);
			\draw [->] (D) to  [bend left =-33] node [above left] {\scriptsize$f$} (A);
			\draw [->] (D)  to [bend left =33] node [above right] {\scriptsize$g$} (C);
			\draw [->] (P) to  node [below] {\scriptsize$\pi_1$} (A);
			\draw [->] (P)  to node [below] {\scriptsize$\pi_2$} (C);
			\node at (2,1.25) {\scriptsize$\mathrm{pb}$};
		\end{tikzpicture}
		\vspace{-0.3cm}
	\end{center}
<!-- end definition-5 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{Barrtheorem}
	For a functor $T : \mathbf{Set} \to \mathbf{Set}$, $T$ preserves weak-pullbacks if and only if the Barr extension is a monotone strict extension and it is the unique one.
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{th:paramBarr}
	For a functor $T$ as above and $i\in \{1,2,3,4\}$, the Barr extension $\mathcal{L}_i$ verifies:
\begin{enumerate}
		\item it is normal: for all $A,B,X$, $\mathrm{id}_{T(A,B,X)} = \mathcal{L}_i(\mathrm{id}_{A}, \mathrm{id}_{B},\mathrm{id}_X)$ 
	\item it is antitone in the first argument and monotone in the last two arguments: for all $M'\leq M : A  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B$, $N\leq N': C  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 D$ and $R\leq R' : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$, 
	\[\mathcal{L}_i(M ,N, R) \leq \mathcal{L}_i(M' ,N',R')\]
	\item it preserves composition laxly in the first argument: for all $M: A_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 A_2$, $M' :A_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 A_3$, 
	\[	\mathcal{L}_i(M,\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}) ; \mathcal{L}_i(M',\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id})  \leq  	\mathcal{L}_i(M; M',\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id})\]
	\item it preserves composition op-laxly in the last two arguments: for all 
	$N: B_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_2$, $N' :B_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_3$, $R: X_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 X_2$ and $R' :X_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 X_3$, 
	\[\mathcal{L}_i(\mathrm{id} ,N; N',R; R') \leq \mathcal{L}_i(\mathrm{id} ,N,R) ; \mathcal{L}_i(\mathrm{id} ,N',R')\]
	\item for all $f : A\to B$, $g : C \to D$ and $h : X \to Y$, 
	\[(T(f, g, h))_* = \mathcal{L}_i(f^*, g_*, h_* )\text{ and }(T(f, g, h))^*=  \mathcal{L}_i(f_*,  g^*, h^* )\]
	\item it is symmetric: for all $M : A  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B$, $N: C  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 D$ and $R : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$, 
	\[(\mathcal{L}_i(M ,N, R))^t = \mathcal{L}_i(M^t ,N^t,R^t)\]
	\item it is minimal: for any lax extension $\mathcal{L}$ of $T$ and relations $M,N,R$, we have \[\mathcal{L}_i(M,N,R) \leq \mathcal{L}(M,N,R).\]
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:WeakPbkLastTwo} For $i\in \{1,2,3,4\}$, if for every weak pullback squares as on the left below,
		\begin{center}
		\begin{tikzpicture}[thick,scale=0.5]
			\node (A) at (0,1.5) {\scriptsize$B$};
			\node (B) at (2,0) {\scriptsize$D$};
			\node (C) at (4,1.5) {\scriptsize$C$};
			\node (D) at (2,3) {\scriptsize$A$};
			
			\draw [->] (A)  to  node [below left] {\scriptsize$h$} (B);
			\draw [->] (C)  to  node [below right] {\scriptsize$k$} (B);
			\draw [->] (D) to  node [above left] {\scriptsize$f$} (A);
			\draw [->] (D)  to node [above right] {\scriptsize$g$} (C);
			\node at (2,1.5) {\scriptsize$\mathrm{wpb}$};
			
			\begin{scope}[xshift=5cm]
				\node (A) at (0,1.5) {\scriptsize$X$};
				\node (B) at (2,0) {\scriptsize$Z$};
				\node (C) at (4,1.5) {\scriptsize$Y$};
				\node (D) at (2,3) {\scriptsize$W$};
				
				\draw [->] (A)  to  node [below left] {\scriptsize$l$} (B);
				\draw [->] (C)  to  node [below right] {\scriptsize$m$} (B);
				\draw [->] (D) to  node [above left] {\scriptsize$i$} (A);
				\draw [->] (D)  to node [above right] {\scriptsize$j$} (C);
				\node at (2,1.5) {\scriptsize$\mathrm{wpb}$};
				
					\node at (6,1.5) {$\Rightarrow$};
			\end{scope}
			
			
				\begin{scope}[xshift=14cm,yscale=1.2, xscale=1.1]
				\node (A) at (0,1.5) {\scriptsize$T(U,B,X)$};
				\node (B) at (2,0) {\scriptsize$T(U,D,Z)$};
				\node (C) at (4,1.5) {\scriptsize$T(U,C,Y)$};
				\node (D) at (2,3) {\scriptsize$T(U,A,W)$};
				
				\draw [->] (A)  to  node [below left] {\scriptsize$T(\mathrm{id}, h,l)$} (B);
				\draw [->] (C)  to  node [below right] {\scriptsize$T(\mathrm{id}, k.m)$} (B);
				\draw [->] (D) to  node [above left] {\scriptsize$T(\mathrm{id}, f,i)$} (A);
				\draw [->] (D)  to node [above right] {\scriptsize$T(\mathrm{id}, g,j)$} (C);
				\node at (2,1.5) {\scriptsize$\mathrm{wpb}$};
			\end{scope}
		\end{tikzpicture}
	\end{center} their image by $T$ as on the right is a weak pullback, then for all 
	$N: B_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_2$, $N' :B_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_3$, $R: X_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 X_2$ and $R' :X_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 X_3$, $ \mathcal{L}_i(\mathrm{id} ,N',R') \circ \mathcal{L}_i(\mathrm{id} ,N,R)  \leq	\mathcal{L}_i(\mathrm{id} ,N'\circ N,R'\circ R)$.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:laxExtParam} We say that $T$ satisfies the \emph{strong interchange property} if for every $f: A \to B$, $g : C \to D$ and $h : X \to Y$, the following square is a weak pullback 
		\begin{center}
		\begin{tikzpicture}[thick,yscale=0.5,xscale=0.7]
		
				\node (A) at (0,1.5) {\scriptsize$T(A,C,X)$};
				\node (B) at (2,0) {\scriptsize$T(A,D,Y)$};
				\node (C) at (4,1.5) {\scriptsize$T(B,D,Y)$};
				\node (D) at (2,3) {\scriptsize$T(B,C,X)$};
				
				\draw [->] (A)  to  node [below left] {\scriptsize$T(\mathrm{id}, g,h)$} (B);
				\draw [->] (C)  to  node [below right] {\scriptsize$T(f, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id})$} (B);
				\draw [->] (D) to  node [above left] {\scriptsize$T(f, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id})$} (A);
				\draw [->] (D)  to node [above right] {\scriptsize$T(\mathrm{id}, g,h)$} (C);
				\node at (2,1.5) {\scriptsize$\mathrm{wpb}$};

		\end{tikzpicture}
	\end{center} 
	If $T$ preserves weak pullbacks in its last two arguments as in Lemma~\ref{lem:WeakPbkLastTwo} and satisfies the strong interchange property, then all the operators $\mathcal{L}_1$, $\mathcal{L}_2$, $\mathcal{L}_3$ and $\mathcal{L}_4$ become equal and are parametrized lax extensions.
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{prop:laxExtParamWeak} We say that $T$ satisfies the \emph{weak interchange property} if for every spans $(g : P \to C, h : P \to D)$ and $(u : Q \to X, v : Q \to Y)$ and split epi $f : A \to B$, the canonical inclusion 
	\[\begin{aligned}
	&(T(\mathrm{id}, g, u ))^* ; (T(f,\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*; (T(f,\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*; (T(\mathrm{id},h, v))_*\leq \\
	&(T(f,\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*; (T(\mathrm{id}, g, u ))^* ; (T(\mathrm{id},h, v))_*(T(f,\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*
	\end{aligned}\]
is an equality. 
	If $T$ preserves weak pullbacks in its last two arguments as in Lemma~\ref{lem:WeakPbkLastTwo} and satisfies the weak interchange property, then $\mathcal{L}_4$ is a parametrized lax extension.
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- definition-6: body_md -->
\label{def:laxextensionmonad}
	 A \emph{lax extension} of a strong monad $(T, \mu, \eta, \mathrm{str})$ on $\mathbf{Set}$ to $\mathbf{Rel}$ consists a lax extension $\overline{T}$ of $T$, as in Definition~\ref{def:laxextension}, satisfying the following axioms:
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item for all $ R : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$, $(\eta_Y)_*  \circ R\leq  \overline{T}(R) \circ (\eta_X)_*$;
		\item for all $ R : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$, $(\mu_Y)_*  \circ \overline{T}(\overline{T}(R)) \leq  \overline{T}(R) \circ (\mu_X)_*$.
		\item for all $R : A  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B$ and $S : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$, $(\mathrm{str}_{B,Y})_* \circ (R \times \overline{T}(S)) \leq \overline{T}(R \times S) \circ \mathrm{str}_{A,X}$.
	\end{enumerate}
	If $\overline{T}$ is a strict extension for $T$ and conditions $1.-3.$ are equalities, we say that $(\overline{T}, \mu_*, \eta_*, \mathrm{str}_*)$ is a \emph{strict extension} for $(T, \mu, \eta, \mathrm{str})$.
<!-- end definition-6 -->
<!-- definition-7: body_md -->
\label{def:LaxParamExtensionMonad}
	For a parametrized monad  $(T ,\eta,\mu)$, a \emph{parametrized lax extension} consists of a lax extensions $\overline{T}$ as in Definition~\ref{def:ParamLaxExtensionMonadSet}, such that the relations $(\eta_{A,X})_*$ and $	(\mu_{A_1,A_2,A_3,X})_*$ verify:
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item for all $M : A  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B$ and $R : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$,
		\[
		R ;	(\eta_{B, Y})_* \leq (\eta_{A, X})_*; \overline{T} (M,M,R)
		\]
		\item for all $M: A_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_1$, $N: A_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_2$, $O: A_3  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_3$ and $R : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$,
		\[
		\overline{T}(M,N, \overline{T}(N, O, R)) ;(\mu_{B_1, B_2, B_3,Y})_*  \leq (\mu_{A_1, A_2,A_3,X})_*;\overline{T}(M,O, R)
		\]
	\end{enumerate}
	If the parametrized monad is equipped with a strength $\mathrm{str}$, then a \emph{strong lax extension for $T$} is a lax extension as above satisfying the additional axiom:
	\begin{enumerate}
		\setcounter{enumi}{2}
		\item for all $M: A_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_1$, $N: A_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_2$, $R : X_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y_1$ and $S : X_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y_2$
		\[
		(R\times \overline{T}(M,N, S) ); (\mathrm{str}_{A_2, B_2, X_2, Y_2})_* \leq (\mathrm{str}_{A_1, B_1, X_1, Y_1})_*;  \overline{T}(M,N, R \times S).
		\]
	\end{enumerate}
	A \emph{strict parametrized extension} consists of a strict extension $\overline{T}$ as in Definition~\ref{def:strictParamExtension} for which the inequalities $1.-3.$ above are equalities.
<!-- end definition-7 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{prop:LaxParamExtensionMonad}
	For a strong parametrized monad  $(T ,\eta,\mu, \mathrm{str})$, if $\mathcal{L}_4$ is a parametrized lax extension for $T$, then $(\mathcal{L}_4, \eta_*, \mu_*, \mathrm{str}_*)$ is a parametrized lax extension for $(T ,\eta,\mu, \mathrm{str})$ as in Definition~\ref{def:LaxParamExtensionMonad}.
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- example-4: body_md -->
The parametrized state monad has underlying functor $T$ as in Example~\ref{ex:paramStateMonad}.1. For sets $A,X$, the unit has components $\eta_{A,X} : X \to (A \times X)^{A}$ given by the function $x \mapsto \lambda a. (a,x)$. For sets $A,B,C$ and $X$, the component of the multiplication $	\mu_{A,B,C,X}	: T(A, B,T(B,C,X)) \to T(A,C,X)$ maps $\varphi  \in (B \times (C \times X)^B)^A$ to $\lambda a. (\pi_2(\varphi(a))(\pi_1(\varphi(a)))$. It is a strong monad: for sets $A,B,X$ and $Y$, the component of the strength $\mathrm{str}_{A,B,X,Y}: X \times (B \times Y)^A \to  (B \times X\times Y)^A $ maps $(x, \psi) \in X \times (B \times Y)^A $ to $ \lambda a. (\pi_1(\psi(a)), x, \pi_2(\psi(a)))$. We obtain a parametrized lax extension $(\mathcal{L}_4, \eta_*, \mu_*, \mathrm{str}_*)$, it corresponds to the monad structure induced from the parametrized adjunction for the (double) cartesian closed structure of $\mathbf{Rel}$.
<!-- end example-4 -->
<!-- definition-8: body_md -->
For coalgebras $(X, \alpha)$ and $(Y, \beta)$ as above, a relation $R : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$ is \emph{sound} for behavioral equivalence if for any $(x,y) \in R$, the states $x$ and $y$ are behaviorally equivalent. It is \emph{complete} if the reverse inclusion holds and it is \emph{fully abstract} if both inclusions hold.
<!-- end definition-8 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
AM-bisimilarity is sound for behavioral equivalence; if $F$ preserves weak-pullbacks, then it is also fully abstract.
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- proposition-4: body_md -->
\label{prop:AMsoundforLax}
	If $R$ is an AM-bisimulation, then it is a $\bar{F}$-bisimulation for any lax extension $\bar{F}$.
<!-- end proposition-4 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:LaxExtFullyAbstract}
	For a lax extension $\bar{F}$, $\bar{F}$-bisimilarity is complete for behavioral equivalence and if $\bar{F}$ is normal, then $\bar{F}$-bisimilarity is fully abstract.
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- definition-9: body_md -->
\label{def:parambisim}
	A relation $R : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$ is a $\overline{T}$-bisimulation w.r.t $(M,N)$ if it satisfies $R \leq  \alpha_* ; \overline{T}(M,N, R) ; \beta^*$.
<!-- end definition-9 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:paramcond}
	If $M \leq \mathrm{id}_A$ and $ \mathrm{id}_C \leq N$, then $\mathrm{id}_X$ is a $\overline{T}$-bisimulation w.r.t $(M,N)$. If $M \leq M \circ M $, $ N \circ N \leq N $ and $R$ is a $\overline{T}$-bisimulation w.r.t $(M,N)$, then so is $R \circ R$.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
Assume that $M^t = M$, $N^t = N$ and $\overline{T}$ is symmetric (axiom $4.$ in Definition~\ref{def:ParamLaxExtensionMonadSet}). If $R$ is a $\overline{T}$-bisimulation w.r.t $(M,N)$, then so is $R^t$.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:AMLaxExtSoundness}
	If $R$ is an AM-bisimulation w.r.t $(M,N)$, then it is a $\overline{T}$-bisimulation w.r.t $(M,N)$.
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:paramBehEquivFinal}
	For $\alpha$ and $(M,N)$ as above, if the functor $T(\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, W, -) : \mathbf{Set} \to \mathbf{Set}$ has a final coalgebra $\omega : \Omega \to T(\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, W, \Omega)$, then two states $(x,y) \in X \times X$ are behaviorally equivalent if and only if $\llbracket x \rrbracket_1 = \llbracket y \rrbracket_2$ where $\llbracket - \rrbracket_1$ and $\llbracket - \rrbracket_2$ are the two unique coalgebra morphisms as below:
	\begin{center}
	\begin{tikzpicture}[thick, xscale=0.9, yscale=0.5]
		\node (A) at (0,0) {\scriptsize$T(A,C,X)$};
		\node (A1) at (0,-2) {\scriptsize$T(\mathbf{gr}{(M)},W,X)$};
		\node (B) at (3,-3) {\scriptsize$T(\mathbf{gr}{(M)},W,\Omega)$};
		\node (C) at (0,2) {\scriptsize$X$};
		\node (D) at (3,1) {\scriptsize$\Omega$};
		\node (E) at (6,2) {\scriptsize$X$};
		\node (F) at (6,0) {\scriptsize$T(A,C,X)$};
		\node (F1) at (6,-2) {\scriptsize$T(\mathbf{gr}{(M)},W,X)$};
		
		\draw [->] (C) -- node [left] {\scriptsize$\alpha$} (A);
		\draw [->] (A1) -- node [below left] {\scriptsize$T(\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id} ,\llbracket - \rrbracket_1)$} (B);
		\draw [->] (A) -- node [left] {\scriptsize$T(\pi_1^M, h,\mathrm{id})$} (A1);
		\draw [->] (F) -- node [ right] {\scriptsize$T(\pi_2^M, k, \mathrm{id})$} (F1);
		\draw [->] (F1) -- node [below right] {\scriptsize$T(\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}, \llbracket - \rrbracket_2)$} (B);
		\draw [->] (C) -- node [above] {\scriptsize$ \llbracket - \rrbracket_1$} (D);
		\draw [->] (E) -- node [above] {\scriptsize$ \llbracket - \rrbracket_2$} (D);
		\draw [->] (E) -- node [right] {\scriptsize$\alpha$} (F);
		\draw [->] (D) -- node [right] {\scriptsize$\omega$} (B);
	\end{tikzpicture}
\end{center}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proposition-5: body_md -->
\label{prop:AMBehEquivsoundness}
	If $T$ preserves weak-pullbacks in the last two variables, then $(M,N)$-behavioral equivalence is sound for AM-bisimilarity w.r.t $(M,N)$.
<!-- end proposition-5 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:laxdblefunctor}
	For relations $M,N,R$ and functions $f,g,h,k,i,j$ as below
	\begin{center}
		\begin{tikzpicture}[thick,xscale=0.4, yscale =0.4, decoration={ markings, mark=at position 0.5 with {\arrow{|}}}]
			\node (A) at (0,3) {$A_1$};
			\node (B) at (4,3) {$A_2$};
			\node (C) at (0,0) {$A_3$};
			\node (D) at (4,0) {$A_4$};
			
			\draw [->] (A) -- node [left] {$f$} (C);
			\draw [->, postaction=decorate] (A) -- node [above] {$M$} (B);
			\draw [->] (B) -- node [right] {$g$} (D);
			\begin{scope}[xshift=8cm]
				\node (A) at (0,3) {$B_1$};
				\node (B) at (4,3) {$B_2$};
				\node (C) at (0,0) {$B_3$};
				\node (D) at (4,0) {$B_4$};
				
				\draw [->] (A) -- node [left] {$h$} (C);
				\draw [->, postaction=decorate] (C) -- node [below] {$N$} (D);
				\draw [->] (B) -- node [right] {$k$} (D);
				
			\end{scope}
			\begin{scope}[xshift=16cm]
				\node (A) at (0,3) {$X_1$};
				\node (B) at (4,3) {$X_2$};
				\node (C) at (0,0) {$X_3$};
				\node (D) at (4,0) {$X_4$};
				
				\draw [->] (A) -- node [left] {$i$} (C);
				\draw [->, postaction=decorate] (C) -- node [below] {$R$} (D);
				\draw [->] (B) -- node [right] {$j$} (D);
			\end{scope}
		\end{tikzpicture}
		
	\end{center}
	if $\overline{T}$ is a parametrized lax extension, then 
	\[
	\overline{T} (f^*;M;g_*, h_*;N;k^*, i_*;R;j^*) = (T(f,h,i))_*; \overline{T} (M, N, R) ;  (T(g,k,j))^*
	\]
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{th:BehEquivExtFullabstract}
	$\overline{T}$-bisimilarity w.r.t $(M,N)$ is complete for $(M,N)$-behavioral equivalence and if $\overline{T}$ is normal, then it is fully abstract.
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
If a function $f: A \to B$ is split epi, then $f^*; f_* =\mathrm{id}_B: B  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B$ and if a function $f: A \to B$ is split mono, then $f_*: f^*=\mathrm{id}_A : A  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 A$.
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
For relations $R : A  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
  B$ and $S : B 
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 C$, if we take the pullback as on the left below (or equivalently the composition of the two tabulation spans), then the unique morphism $u: P \to \mathbf{gr}{(S \circ R)}$ as on the right is split epi.
	\begin{center}
		\begin{tikzpicture}[thick,xscale=0.7, yscale=0.7]
			\node (A) at (2.5,0) {$A$};
			\node (Y1) at (4,1.5) {$\mathbf{gr}{(R)}$};
			\node (B) at (5.5,0) {$B$};
			\draw [->] (Y1)  to node [above left] {$\pi_1^R$} (A);
			\draw [->] (Y1)  to node [below left] {$\pi_2^R$} (B);
			
			\node (X2) at (7,1.5) {$\mathbf{gr}{(S)}$};
			\node (P) at (5.5,3) {$P$};
			\node (C) at (8.5,0) {$C$};
			\draw [->] (P) to node [above left] {$p_R$} (Y1);
			\draw [->] (P) to node [above right] {$p_S$} (X2);
			\draw [->] (X2)  to node [below right] {$\pi_1^S$} (B);
			\draw [->] (X2)  to node [above right] {$\pi_2^S$} (C);

			\node at (5.5,1.5) {$\mathrm{pb}$};
			
			\begin{scope}[xshift=10cm]
	\node (A) at (2.5,0) {$A$};
\node (Y1) at (3.5,1.5) {$\mathbf{gr}{(R)}$};
\node (B) at (5.5,0.8) {$\mathbf{gr}{(S\circ R)}$};
\draw [->] (Y1)  to node [above left] {$\pi_1^R$} (A);
\draw [->] (B)  to node [below] {$\pi_1^{S\circ R}$} (A);

\node (X2) at (7.5,1.5) {$\mathbf{gr}{(S)}$};
\node (P) at (5.5,3) {$P$};
\node (C) at (8.5,0) {$C$};
\draw [->] (P) to node [above left] {$p_R$} (Y1);
\draw [->, dotted] (P) to node [ left] {$u$} (B);
\draw [->] (P) to node [above right] {$p_S$} (X2);
\draw [->] (B)  to node [below] {$\pi_1^{S\circ R}$} (C);
\draw [->] (X2)  to node [above right] {$\pi_2^S$} (C);

			\end{scope}

	\end{center}
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
We will only prove the theorem for $\mathcal{L}_1$, the proofs for $i={2,3,4}$ follow the same pattern.
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item[]
	\item To obtain that $\mathcal{L}_1$ is normal, we use the fact that the tabulation of the identity relation is isomorphic to the identity span.
	\item If $M' \leq M : A  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B$, $N\leq N': C  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 D$ and $R \leq R' : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$, then there exist functions $\alpha: \mathbf{gr}{(M')}\to \mathbf{gr}{(M)}$, $\beta : \mathbf{gr}{(N)} \to \mathbf{gr}{(N')}$ and $\gamma : \mathbf{gr}{(R)} \to \mathbf{gr}{(R)}'$ such that $\pi_1^{M} \circ \alpha = \pi_1^{M'}$, $\pi_2^{M} \circ \alpha = \pi_2^{M'}$, $\pi_1^{N'} \circ \beta = \pi_1^N$, $\pi_2^{N'} \circ \beta = \pi_2^N$, $\pi_1^{R'} \circ \gamma = \pi_1^R$ and $\pi_2^{R'} \circ \gamma = \pi_2^R$. We obtain:
	\[	\begin{aligned}
		&\mathcal{L}_1(M,N,R)
		=(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^N, \pi_1^R))^*;
		(T(\pi_1^{M}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(\pi_2^{M}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^N, \pi_2^R))_* \\
		\leq&(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^N, \pi_1^R))^*;
		(T(\pi_1^{M}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(\alpha, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ;\\
		&\qquad\qquad\qquad (T(\alpha, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ;(T(\pi_2^{M}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^N, \pi_2^R))_* \\
		=&(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^N, \pi_1^R))^*;
		(T(\pi_1^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(\pi_2^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^N, \pi_2^R))_* \\
		=&(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{N'}, \pi_1^{R'}))^*;(T(\mathrm{id}, \beta, \gamma))^*;
		(T(\pi_1^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;\\
		&\qquad\qquad\qquad (T(\pi_2^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \beta, \gamma))_*;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^{N'}, \pi_2^{R'}))_* \\
		\leq & (T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{N'}, \pi_1^{R'}))^*;
		(T(\pi_1^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \beta, \gamma))^*;\\
		&\qquad\qquad\qquad 
		  (T(\mathrm{id}, \beta, \gamma))_*;(T(\pi_2^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^{N'}, \pi_2^{R'}))_* \\
		\leq & (T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{N'}, \pi_1^{R'}))^*;
		(T(\pi_1^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ;(T(\pi_2^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^{N'}, \pi_2^{R'}))_* \\
		=&	\mathcal{L}_1(M',N',R')\\
	\end{aligned}\]
	
	
	
	
	\item for $M: A_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 A_2$, $M' :A_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 A_3$, we have:
	\begin{center}
		\begin{tikzpicture}[thick,xscale=0.7, yscale=0.7]
			\node (A) at (2.5,0) {$A_1$};
			\node (Y1) at (4,1.5) {$\mathbf{gr}{(M)}$};
			\node (B) at (5.5,0) {$A_2$};
			\draw [->] (Y1)  to node [above left] {$\pi_1^{M}$} (A);
			\draw [->] (Y1)  to node [below left] {$\pi_2^M$} (B);
			
			\node (X2) at (7,1.5) {$\mathbf{gr}{(M')}$};
			\node (P) at (5.5,3) {$P_{M,M'}$};
			\node (C) at (8.5,0) {$A_3$};
			\draw [->] (P) to node [above left] {$p_M$} (Y1);
			\draw [->] (P) to node [above right] {$p_{M'}$} (X2);
			\draw [->] (X2)  to node [below right] {$\pi_1^M$} (B);
			\draw [->] (X2)  to node [above right] {$\pi_2^{M'}$} (C);
			
			\node at (5.5,1.5) {pb};
			
			\begin{scope}[xshift=10cm]
				\node (A) at (2.5,0) {$A_1$};
				\node (Y1) at (3.5,1.5) {$\mathbf{gr}{(M)}$};
				\node (B) at (5.5,0.8) {$\mathbf{gr}{(M; M')}$};
				\draw [->] (Y1)  to node [above left] {$\pi_1^M$} (A);
				\draw [->] (B)  to node [below] {$\pi_1^{M; M'}$} (A);
				
				\node (X2) at (7.5,1.5) {$\mathbf{gr}{(M')}$};
				\node (P) at (5.5,3) {$P_{M; M'}$};
				\node (C) at (8.5,0) {$A_3$};
				\draw [->] (P) to node [above left] {$p_{M'}$} (Y1);
				\draw [->, dotted] (P) to node [] {$u_{M,M'}$} (B);
				\draw [->] (P) to node [above right] {$p_{M'}$} (X2);
				\draw [->] (B)  to node [below] {$\pi_1^{M; M'}$} (C);
				\draw [->] (X2)  to node [above right] {$\pi_2^{M'}$} (C);
				
			\end{scope}
		\end{tikzpicture}
	\end{center}
		\[\begin{aligned}
	&\mathcal{L}_1(M,\mathrm{id}_C, \mathrm{id}_X);	\mathcal{L}_1(M',\mathrm{id}_C, \mathrm{id}_X)\\
	&= 	
	(T(\pi_1^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(\pi_2^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*;(T(\pi_1^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(\pi_2^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* \\
	&\leq (T(\pi_1^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(p_M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*;(T(p_{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*  ;(T(\pi_2^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* \\
	&=(T(\pi_1^{M; M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(u_{M,M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*;(T(u_{M,M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*  ;(T(\pi_2^{M; M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* \\
	&=(T(\pi_1^{M; M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*   ;(T(\pi_2^{M; M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* =	\mathcal{L}_1(M; M',\mathrm{id}_C, \mathrm{id}_X)
\end{aligned}\]
	\item the proof for $\mathcal{L}_1(\mathrm{id} ,N'\circ N,R'\circ R) \leq \mathcal{L}_1(\mathrm{id} ,N',R') \circ \mathcal{L}_1(\mathrm{id} ,N,R)  $	is similar.

		\item for $f : A\to B$, $g : C \to D$ and $h : X \to Y$, we have  
		 \begin{center}
			\begin{tikzpicture}[thick,scale=0.5]
				\node (A) at (0,1.5) {

		where $\pi_2^{f^*}$, $\pi_1^{g_*}$ and $\pi_1^{h_*}$ are split epi. We obtain:
			\[\begin{aligned}
			&\mathcal{L}_1(f^*, g_*, h_* )= (T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{g_*}, \pi_1^{h_*}))^* ; (T(\pi_1^{f^*}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ;(T(\pi_2^{f^*}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*	;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^{g_*}, \pi_2^{h_*}))_*  \\
			&= 	(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{g_*}, \pi_1^{h_*}))^*;(T(f, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ;(T(\pi_2^{f^*}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ;\\
			&\qquad\qquad\qquad(T(\pi_2^{f^*}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{g_*}, \pi_1^{h_*}))_* ;T(\mathrm{id}, g, h))_* \\
			&= (T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{g_*}, \pi_1^{h_*}))^*;(T(f, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{g_*}, \pi_1^{h_*}))_* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, g, h))_* \\
			&= (T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{g_*}, \pi_1^{h_*}))^*;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{g_*}, \pi_1^{h_*}))_*;(T(f, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(\mathrm{id}, g, h))_* \\
			&= (T(f, g, h))_*
		\end{aligned}\]
		The proof for $	(T(f, g, h))^*=  \mathcal{L}_1(f_*,  g^*, h^* )$ is similar.

				\draw [->] (B)  to  node [above left] {\scriptsize$\pi_1^{R^t}$} (A);
				\draw [->] (B)  to  node [above right] {\scriptsize$\pi_2^{R^t}$} (C);	
				\draw [->] (D) to  node [below left] {\scriptsize$\pi_2^{R}$} (A);
				\draw [->] (D)  to node [below right] {\scriptsize$\pi_1^{R}$} (C);

		We obtain:
		\[	\begin{aligned}
			\mathcal{L}_1(M^t,N^t,R^t) &=(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^N, \pi_2^R))^* ;
			(T(\pi_2^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ;(T(\pi_1^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^N, \pi_1^R))_* \\
			&=\left((T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^N, \pi_1^R))^* ;(T(\pi_1^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ;(T(\pi_2^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^N, \pi_2^R))_*  \right)^t\\
			&= 	(\mathcal{L}_1(M,N,R))^t
		\end{aligned}\]

		 
		\item let $\mathcal{L}$ be an abitrary lax extension for $T$, for relations $M : A  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B$, $N: C  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 D$ and $R : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$, we have:
\begin{align*}
			&\mathcal{L}_1(M,N,R)=(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^N, \pi_1^R))^*;
			(T(\pi_1^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(\pi_2^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^N, \pi_2^R))_* \\
			&\leq \mathcal{L}(\mathrm{id}, (\pi_1^N)^*, (\pi_1^R)^*) ; \mathcal{L}((\pi_1^M)^* \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}) ; \mathcal{L}((\pi_2^M)_*, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id});\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{id}, (\pi_2^N)_*, (\pi_2^R)_*) \\
			&\leq \mathcal{L}((\pi_1^M)^*;(\pi_2^M)_*, (\pi_1^N)^*; (\pi_2^N)_* (\pi_1^R)^*;(\pi_2^R)_*) = \mathcal{L}(M,N,R)\qedhere
		\end{align*}
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
It is straightforward to obtain $\mathcal{L}_1=\mathcal{L}_2=\mathcal{L}_3=\mathcal{L}_4$ from the strong interchange property. We show that $\mathcal{L}_1$ satisfies axiom $(2)$ in Definition~\ref{def:ParamLaxExtensionMonadSet}:
		\item For relations $M: A_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 A_2$, $M' :A_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 A_3$,  $N: B_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_2$, $N' :B_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_3$, $R: X_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 X_2$ and $R' :X_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 X_3$, we have:
			\[	\begin{aligned}
			&\mathcal{L}_1(M,N,R);  \mathcal{L}_1(M,'N',R')\\
			=&(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^N, \pi_1^R))^*;
			(T(\pi_1^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(\pi_2^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^N, \pi_2^R))_* \\
			&\qquad;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{N'}, \pi_1^{R'}))^*;
			(T(\pi_1^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(\pi_2^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^{N'}, \pi_2^{R'}))_* \\
			=&(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^N, \pi_1^R))^*;
			(T(\pi_1^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(\pi_2^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* 
			;(T(\mathrm{id}, p_N, p_R))^*\\
			&\qquad ;(T(\mathrm{id}, p_{N'}, p_{R'}))_*;
			(T(\pi_1^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(\pi_2^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^{N'}, \pi_2^{R'}))_* \\
			=&T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^N, \pi_1^R))^*;
			(T(\pi_1^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  
			;(T(\mathrm{id}, p_N, p_R))^* ;
						(T(\pi_2^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*\\
						&\qquad ;
			(T(\pi_1^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;
				(T(\mathrm{id}, p_{N'}, p_{R'}))_*;
			(T(\pi_2^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^{N'}, \pi_2^{R'}))_* \\
			\leq &T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^N, \pi_1^R))^*;
			(T(\pi_1^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  
			;(T(\mathrm{id}, p_N, p_R))^* ;
				(T(p_M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*\\
				&\qquad ; 	(T(p_{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ;
			(T(\mathrm{id}, p_{N'}, p_{R'}))_*;
			(T(\pi_2^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^{N'}, \pi_2^{R'}))_* \\
				= &T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^N, \pi_1^R))^*;
			(T(\mathrm{id}, p_N, p_R))^* ;
			(T(\pi_1^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ;
			(T(p_M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* \\
			&\qquad; 	(T(p_{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ;
			(T(\pi_2^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ;
			(T(\mathrm{id}, p_{N'}, p_{R'}))_*;
			(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^{N'}, \pi_2^{R'}))_* \\
			=& 	(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{N;N'}, \pi_1^{R;R'}))^*;(T(\pi_1^{M; M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*   ;(T(\pi_2^{M; M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ; 	(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^{N;N'}, \pi_2^{R;R'}))_*
		\end{aligned}\]
					We use that $T$ preserves pullbacks in the last two variables for the second equality and that the interchange squares are weak pullbacks for the penultimate equality. \qedhere
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
We show that $\mathcal{L}_4$ satisfies axiom $(2)$ in Definition~\ref{def:ParamLaxExtensionMonadSet}:
		\item For relations $M: A_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 A_2$, $M' :A_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 A_3$,  $N: B_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_2$, $N' :B_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_3$, $R: X_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 X_2$ and $R' :X_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 X_3$, we have:
		\[	\begin{aligned}
			&\mathcal{L}_4(M,N,R); \mathcal{L}_4(M,'N',R')\\
			=&	(T(\pi_1^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^N, \pi_1^R))^*;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^N, \pi_2^R))_* ;
			(T(\pi_2^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* \\
			&\qquad;(T(\pi_1^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{N'}, \pi_1^{R'}))^*;
		(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^{N'}, \pi_2^{R'}))_*;	(T(\pi_2^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*  \\
		\leq &	(T(\pi_1^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^N, \pi_1^R))^*;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^N, \pi_2^R))_* ;
		(T(p_M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* \\
		&\qquad;(T(p_{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*  ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{N'}, \pi_1^{R'}))^*;
		(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^{N'}, \pi_2^{R'}))_*;	(T(\pi_2^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*  \\
		= &	(T(\pi_1^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^N, \pi_1^R))^*;(T(p_M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^N, \pi_2^R))_* ;
		\\
		&\qquad ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{N'}, \pi_1^{R'}))^*;(T(p_{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ;
		(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^{N'}, \pi_2^{R'}))_*;	(T(\pi_2^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*  \\
		\leq &	(T(\pi_1^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ;(T(p_M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^N, \pi_1^R))^*;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^N, \pi_2^R))_* ;
		\\
		&\qquad ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{N'}, \pi_1^{R'}))^* ;
		(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^{N'}, \pi_2^{R'}))_*;	(T(p_{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*;(T(\pi_2^{M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*  \\
		= &	(T(\pi_1^{M; M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(u_{M,M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*;
		T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{N;N'}, \pi_1^{R;R'}))_*	\\
		&\qquad ;	T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^{N;N'}, \pi_2^{R;R'}))_*
		(T(u_{M,M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*  ;
		(T(\pi_2^{M; M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*\\
		= &	(T(\pi_1^{M; M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;
		T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{N;N'}, \pi_1^{R;R'}))_*;(T(u_{M,M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*	\\
		&\qquad ;	
		(T(u_{M,M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*  ; T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^{N;N'}, \pi_2^{R;R'}))_*;
		(T(\pi_2^{M; M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*\\
		= 	&	(T(\pi_1^{M; M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;
		T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^{N;N'}, \pi_1^{R;R'}))_*; T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^{N;N'}, \pi_2^{R;R'}))_*;
		(T(\pi_2^{M; M'}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*\\
		=&	\mathcal{L}_4(M; M',N;N', R;R')
		\end{aligned}\]
		We use that $T$ preserves pullbacks in the last two variables for the third equality and the weak interchange property for the fourth equality. \qedhere
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
\begin{itemize}
		\item[]
		\item Unit: for all $M :A  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B$ and $R : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$. we have
		\[\begin{aligned}
		R ;	(\eta_{B, Y})_* &=(\pi_1^R)^* ; (\pi_2^R)_* ; (\eta_{B, Y})_* \\
		&\leq (\pi_1^R)^* ; (\eta_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, \mathbf{gr}{(R)}})_*; (\eta_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, \mathbf{gr}{(R)}})^*;(\pi_2^R)_* ; (\eta_{B, Y})_*\\
		&\leq  (\eta_{A, X})_*;(T(\pi_1^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^M, \pi_1^R))^* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^M, \pi_2^R))_* ;(T(\pi_2^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*\\
		&= (\eta_{A, X})_*;	\mathcal{L}_4(M,M,R)
		\end{aligned}\]
		\item Multiplication: we want to show that for all $M: A_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_1$, $N: A_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_2$, $O: A_3  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_3$ and $R : X  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y$, the following holds:
		\[	\mathcal{L}_4(M,N,\mathcal{L}_4(N, O, R)) ;(\mu_{B_1, B_2, B_3,Y})_*
	 \leq (\mu_{A_1, A_2,A_3,X})_*;\mathcal{L}_4(M,O, R)
		\]
	Since $\mathcal{L}_4$ is a lax extension, the two variable operator $\mathcal{L}_4(\mathrm{id}, -, -)$ preserves composition strictly. Indeed, we have $\mathcal{L}_4(\mathrm{id}, N; N', R; R') \geq \mathcal{L}_4(\mathrm{id}, N, R) ;\mathcal{L}_4(\mathrm{id}, N', R')$ because it is a lax extension, and by construction, we always have $\mathcal{L}_4(\mathrm{id}, N; N', R; R') \leq \mathcal{L}_4(\mathrm{id}, N, R) ;\mathcal{L}_4(\mathrm{id}, N', R')$ (Theorem~\ref{th:paramBarr}.4). 
	Therefore, using Lemma~\ref{lem:laxdblefunctor}, the desired inequality above becomes:
	\[\begin{aligned}
	 &(T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id})))_* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1, T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1,\pi_1)))^* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2,T( \mathrm{id}, \pi_2, \pi_2)))_* ;\\
	 &\qquad \qquad \qquad \qquad(T(\pi_2, \mathrm{id},T( \pi_2, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id})))^*;(\mu_{B_1, B_2, B_3,Y})_* \\
	 &\leq (\mu_{A_1, A_2,A_3,X})_*;(T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1, \pi_1))^* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2, \pi_2))_* ;(T(\pi_2, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*
	\end{aligned}\]
	We will combine the following two inequalities to establish the desired inequality above:
	\begin{align*}
&(T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id})))_* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1, T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1,\pi_1)))^* ;(\mu_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, \mathbf{gr}{(N)},\mathbf{gr}{(O)},\mathbf{gr}{(R)}})_* \\
&\leq (\mu_{A_1, A_2,A_3,X})_*;(T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1, \pi_1))^* \tag{$\star$} \label{ineq1} 
	\end{align*}
	\begin{align*}
		&(\mu_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, \mathbf{gr}{(N)},\mathbf{gr}{(O)},\mathbf{gr}{(R)}})^*; (T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2,T( \mathrm{id}, \pi_2, \pi_2)))_* ;(T(\pi_2, \mathrm{id},T( \pi_2, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id})))^*;\\
		& \qquad(\mu_{B_1, B_2, B_3,Y})_* \\
	&\leq	(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2, \pi_2))_* ;(T(\pi_2, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* \tag{$\star\star$} \label{ineq2} 
	\end{align*}
	\begin{itemize}
		\item proof of (\ref{ineq1}) 
		\[\begin{aligned}
			&(T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id})))_* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1, T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1,\pi_1)))^* ;(\mu_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, \mathbf{gr}{(N)},\mathbf{gr}{(O)},\mathbf{gr}{(R)}})_* \\
			=&(T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ; (T(\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}, T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id})))_*		;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1, \mathrm{id}))^*	;(T(\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}, T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1,\pi_1)))^* ;\\
			&\qquad (\mu_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, \mathbf{gr}{(N)},\mathbf{gr}{(O)},\mathbf{gr}{(R)}})_* \\
			=&(T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* 	;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1, \mathrm{id}))^*	; (T(\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}, T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id})))_*	;(T(\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}, T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1,\pi_1)))^* ;\\
			&\qquad (\mu_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, \mathbf{gr}{(N)},\mathbf{gr}{(O)},\mathbf{gr}{(R)}})_* \\
			=\leq&(T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* 	;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1, \mathrm{id}))^*	; (T(\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}, T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id})))_*	; (\mu_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, \mathbf{gr}{(N)},A_3,X})_*  ; \\
			&\qquad (T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1,\pi_1))^* \\
			\leq&(T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* 	;(\mu_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)},A_2,A_3,X})_*; (T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1,\pi_1))^* \\
			\leq & (\mu_{A_1, A_2,A_3,X})_*;(T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1, \pi_1))^*
		\end{aligned}
		\]
		\item proof of (\ref{ineq2}) 
		\[\begin{aligned}
			&(\mu_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, \mathbf{gr}{(N)},\mathbf{gr}{(O)},\mathbf{gr}{(R)}})^*; (T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2,T( \mathrm{id}, \pi_2, \pi_2)))_* ;(T(\pi_2, \mathrm{id},T( \pi_2, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id})))^*;\\
			& \qquad(\mu_{B_1, B_2, B_3,Y})_* \\
			=& (\mu_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, \mathbf{gr}{(N)},\mathbf{gr}{(O)},\mathbf{gr}{(R)}})^*; 	(T(\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id},T( \mathrm{id}, \pi_2, \pi_2)))_* ;	(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2,\mathrm{id}))_* ;\\
			& \qquad 	(T(\mathrm{id} ,\mathrm{id},T( \pi_2, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id})))^*;	(T(\pi_2, \mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})))^*;(\mu_{B_1, B_2, B_3,Y})_* \\
			=& (\mu_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, \mathbf{gr}{(N)},\mathbf{gr}{(O)},\mathbf{gr}{(R)}})^*; 	(T(\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id},T( \mathrm{id}, \pi_2, \pi_2)))_* ;	(T(\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id},T( \pi_2, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id})))^*;\\
			& \qquad 	(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2,\mathrm{id}))_*	;	(T(\pi_2, \mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})))^*;(\mu_{B_1, B_2, B_3,Y})_* \\
			\leq& (\mu_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, \mathbf{gr}{(N)},\mathbf{gr}{(O)},\mathbf{gr}{(R)}})^*; 	(T(\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id},T( \mathrm{id}, \pi_2, \pi_2)))_* ;	(T(\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id},T( \pi_2, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id})))^*;\\
			& \qquad 	(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2,\mathrm{id}))_*	;(\mu_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, B_2, B_3,Y})_* ;	(T(\pi_2, \mathrm{id},\mathrm{id}))^*\\
			\leq& (\mu_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, \mathbf{gr}{(N)},\mathbf{gr}{(O)},\mathbf{gr}{(R)}})^*; 	(T(\mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id},T( \mathrm{id}, \pi_2, \pi_2)))_* ;	(\mu_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, \mathbf{gr}{(N)}, B_3,Y})_*;	(T(\pi_2, \mathrm{id},\mathrm{id}))^*\\
			\leq&	(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2, \pi_2))_* ;(T(\pi_2, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* \
		\end{aligned}
		\]
	\end{itemize}
	In the proofs of (\ref{ineq1}) and (\ref{ineq2}), the second equality uses the fact that $\mathcal{L}_4(\mathrm{id}, -, -)$ preserves composition strictly. 
	\item Strength: for all $M: A_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_1$, $N: A_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 B_2$, $R : X_1  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y_1$ and $S : X_2  
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 Y_2$, we have 
	\[\begin{aligned}
			&(R\times \mathcal{L}_4(M,N, S)) ; (\mathrm{str}_{A_2, B_2, X_2, Y_2})_*\\
			=& (\mathrm{id} \times (T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ) ;((\pi_1)^* \times (T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1, \pi_1))^*);((\pi_2)_* \times T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2, \pi_2))_* ;\\
			&\qquad (\mathrm{id} \times (T(\pi_2, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^*);( \mathrm{str}_{A_2, B_2, X_2, Y_2})_* \\
			\leq& (\mathrm{id} \times (T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ) ;((\pi_1)^* \times (T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1, \pi_1))^*);((\pi_2)_* \times T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2, \pi_2))_* ;\\
			&\qquad ( \mathrm{str}_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, B_2, X_2, Y_2})_* ;(T(\pi_2, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}\times \mathrm{id}))^*\\
			\leq& (\mathrm{id} \times (T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ) ;((\pi_1)^* \times (T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1, \pi_1))^*);( \mathrm{str}_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, \mathbf{gr}{(N)}, \mathbf{gr}{(R)}, \mathbf{gr}{(S)}})_*; \\
			&\qquad ( T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2, \pi_2 \times \pi_2))_*;(T(\pi_2, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}\times \mathrm{id}))^*\\
			\leq& (\mathrm{id} \times (T(\pi_1, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_* ) ; ( \mathrm{str}_{\mathbf{gr}{(M)},B_1,X_1,Y_1})_* ;	(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1, \pi_1 \times \pi_1))^*; \\
			&\qquad ( T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2, \pi_2 \times \pi_2))_*;(T(\pi_2, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}\times \mathrm{id}))^*\\
			\leq& ( \mathrm{str}_{A_1,B_1,X_1,Y_1})_*; (T(\pi_1 , \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id} \times \mathrm{id}))_*   ;	(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1, \pi_1 \times \pi_1))^*; \\
&\qquad ( T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2, \pi_2 \times \pi_2))_*;(T(\pi_2, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}\times \mathrm{id}))^*\\
=& (\mathrm{str}_{A_1, B_1, X_1, Y_1})_*;  \mathcal{L}_4(M,N, R \times S).
	\end{aligned}\]
		\end{itemize}

<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Assume that we have an AM-bisimulation w.r.t $(M,N)$ as in Section~\ref{sec:AMbisim}, then we obtain that it is a $\overline{T}$-bisimulation w.r.t $(M,N)$ from the axioms of parametrized lax extension:
	\begin{align*}
		&(\pi_1^R)^*; 	(\pi_2^R)_* \\
		&\leq\alpha_* ;
		(T(\pi_1^{M}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))_*  ;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^N, \pi_1^R))^*;(T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^N, \pi_2^R))_*;(T(\pi_2^{M}, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}))^* ; \beta^* \\
		&\leq \alpha_* ;
		\overline{T}((\pi_1^{M})^*, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id})  ;	\overline{T}(\mathrm{id}, (\pi_1^N)^*, (\pi_1^R)^*);	\overline{T}(\mathrm{id}, (\pi_2^N)_*, (\pi_2^R)_*);\overline{T}((\pi_2^{M})_*, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id}) ; \alpha^*\\
		&\leq \alpha_* ; \overline{T}(M,N,R); \beta^* \qedhere
	\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
\begin{itemize}
		\item[]
		\item Assume that $(x,y)$ are behaviorally equivalent. Let $\llbracket - \rrbracket_3: Z \to \Omega$ be the unique coalgebra morphism from $\gamma$ to $\omega$. By uniqueness, we obtain $\llbracket - \rrbracket_1 = \llbracket - \rrbracket_3 \circ f$ and $\llbracket - \rrbracket_2= \llbracket - \rrbracket_3\circ g$. Since $(x,y) \in \mathbf{gr}{(R)}$ and $f(x) = g(y)$, we obtain $\llbracket x \rrbracket_1 = \llbracket y \rrbracket_2$.
		\item For the other direction, assume that $\llbracket x \rrbracket_1 = \llbracket y \rrbracket_2$. Let $(P, p_1, p_2)$ be the following pullback:
			\begin{center}
			\begin{tikzpicture}[thick,scale=0.5]
			
					\node (A) at (0,1.5) {$X$};
					\node (B) at (2,0) {$Z$};
					\node (C) at (4,1.5) {$X$};
					\node (D) at (2,3) {$P$};
					
					\draw [->] (A)  to  node [below left] {\scriptsize$f$} (B);
					\draw [->] (C)  to  node [below right] {\scriptsize$g$} (B);
					\draw [->] (D) to  node [above left] {\scriptsize$p_1$} (A);
					\draw [->] (D)  to node [above right] {\scriptsize$p_2$} (C);
					\node at (2,1.5) {\scriptsize$\mathrm{pb}$};

			\end{tikzpicture}
		\end{center}
		and define $R := f_*; g^*$. From the universal property of tabulators, we obtain that the span $(P, p_1, p_2)$ is isomorphic to $(\mathbf{gr}{(R)}, \pi_1^R, \pi_2^R)$ which finishes the proof.\qedhere
	\end{itemize}
<!-- end proof-6 -->

	We want to show that $R$ is an AM-bisimulation w.r.t $(M,N)$. Since $T$ preserves weak pullbacks in the last two variables, the square below is a weak pullback.
	\begin{center}
		\begin{tikzpicture}[thick,xscale=0.7, yscale=0.8]
			\node (A) at (0,1.5) {\scriptsize$T(\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, C,X)$};

			\node (C) at (4,1.5) {\scriptsize$T(\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, C,X)$};
			\node (D) at (2,3) {\scriptsize$T(\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, \mathbf{gr}{(N)},\mathbf{gr}{(R)})$};
			
			\draw [->] (A)  to  node [below left] {\scriptsize$T(\mathrm{id}, h,f)$} (B);
			\draw [->] (C)  to  node [below right] {\scriptsize$T(\mathrm{id}, k,g)$} (B);
			\draw [->] (D) to  node [above left] {\scriptsize$T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_1^N,  \pi_1^R)$} (A);
			\draw [->] (D)  to node [above right] {\scriptsize$T(\mathrm{id}, \pi_2^N,  \pi_2^R)$} (C);
			\node at (2,1.5) {\scriptsize$\mathrm{wpb}$};
			
		\end{tikzpicture}
	\end{center}
	Note that we see here why both $N$ and $R$ are assumed to be difunctional.
	By the universal property of weak-pullbacks, since the diagram below commutes,

		\begin{tikzpicture}[thick, xscale=0.9, yscale=0.6]

			\draw [->] (F) -- node [ right] {\scriptsize$T(\pi_2^M, \mathrm{id}, \mathrm{id} )$} (F1);
			\draw [->] (F1) -- node [below right] {\scriptsize$T(\mathrm{id}, k,g)$} (B);

		\end{tikzpicture}

	 there exists a morphism $\gamma : \mathbf{gr}{(R)} \to T(\mathbf{gr}{(M)}, \mathbf{gr}{(N)},\mathbf{gr}{(R)}) $ such that:

We show both inclusions:
	\begin{align*}
			(T(f,h,i))_*; \overline{T} (M, N, R) ;  (T(g,k,j))^* &\leq 
			\overline{T} (f^*,h_*,i_*); \overline{T} (M, N, R) ;  \overline{T} (g_*,k^*,j^*) \\
			& \leq \overline{T} (f^*;M;g_*, h_*;N;k^*, i_*;R;j^*)
	\end{align*}
		\begin{align*}
			&\overline{T} (f^*;M;g_*, h_*;N;k^*, i_*;R;j^*) \\
			&\leq (T(f,h,i))_*; (T(f,h,i))^*; \overline{T} (f^*;M;g_*, h_*;N;k^*, i_*;R;j^*) ; (T(g,k,j))_*; (T(g,k,j))^*\\
				&\leq (T(f,h,i))_*; \overline{T}(f_*,h^*,i^*); \overline{T} (f^*;M;g_*, h_*;N;k^*, i_*;R;j^*) ; \overline{T}(g^*,k_*,j_*); (T(g,k,j)^*\\
				&\leq (T(f,h,i))_*; \overline{T} (f_*;f^*;M;g_*;g^*, h^*;h_*;N;k^*;k_*, i^*i_*;R;j^*;j_*);(T(g,k,j))^*\\
				&\leq 	(T(f,h,i))_*; \overline{T} (M, N, R) ;  (T(g,k,j))^*\qedhere
		\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
\begin{itemize}
		\item[]
		\item 
	Assume that $R$ is an $(M,N)$-behavioral equivalence as in Section~\ref{subsec:paramBehequiv}, then we obtain that it is a $\overline{T}$-bisimulation w.r.t $(M,N)$:
	\[\begin{aligned}
		f_*; g^* &\leq  	\alpha_* ;  	(T(\pi_1, h,f))_*;(T(\pi_2, k,g))^* ; \alpha^*\\
		& \leq 
		\alpha_* ; 
		\overline{T}((\pi_1)^*, h_*,f_*);\overline{T}((\pi_2)_*, k^*,g^*) ; \alpha^*\\
		&=  \alpha_*;	\overline{T}(M,N,R); \alpha^*
	\end{aligned}
	\]
	\item For the other direction, we want to show that $\overline{T}$-bisimilarity w.r.t $(M,N)$ is an $(M,N)$-behavioral equivalence.

	The operator $\mathbf{Rel}(X,X) \to \mathbf{Rel}(X,X)$ mapping $R : X 
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 X$ to $\alpha_* ; \overline{T}(M, N, R) ; \alpha^*$ is monotone in $R$ so it has a greatest fixpoint which we denote $E : X 
	\mathrel{ 
		\tikz{\draw[module,->,transform canvas={yshift=3pt} 
			] (0,0) -- node[above]{$\scriptstyle \null$} ++(1/2,0) ; 
		}
	} 
 X$. If $M \leq \mathrm{id}_{A}$,  $M \leq M \circ M$, $M \leq M^t $, $\mathrm{id}_C \leq N$, $ N \circ N\leq N$, $N^t \leq N$ and $\overline{T}$ verifies axiom $(6)$ in Definition~\ref{def:ParamLaxExtensionMonadSet}, then $E$ is an equivalence relation (in particular it is difunctional). Since $N$ and $E$ are difunctional, taking the collage of $N$ and $E$, the two squares below are both pushout and weak pullback squares.
	
	\begin{center}
		\begin{tikzpicture}[thick,scale=0.7]
			\node (A) at (0,1.5) {$C$};
			\node (B) at (2,0) {$\mathbf{cl}{(N)}$};
			\node (C) at (4,1.5) {$C$};
			\node (D) at (2,3) {$\mathbf{gr}{(N)}$};
			
			\draw [->] (A)  to  node [below left] {\scriptsize$\iota_1^N$} (B);
			\draw [->] (C)  to  node [below right] {\scriptsize$\iota_2^N$} (B);
			\draw [->] (D) to  node [above left] {\scriptsize$\pi_1^N$} (A);
			\draw [->] (D)  to node [above right] {\scriptsize$\pi_2^N$} (C);
			\node at (2,1.5) {$\mathrm{wpb}/\mathrm{psht}$};
			
			\begin{scope}[xshift=7cm]
				\node (A) at (0,1.5) {$X$};
				\node (B) at (2,0) {$\mathbf{cl}{(E)}$};
				\node (C) at (4,1.5) {$X$};
				\node (D) at (2,3) {$\mathbf{gr}{(E)}$};
				
				\draw [->] (A)  to  node [below left] {\scriptsize$\iota_1^E$} (B);
				\draw [->] (C)  to  node [below right] {\scriptsize$\iota_2^E$} (B);
				\draw [->] (D) to  node [above left] {\scriptsize$\pi_1^E$} (A);
				\draw [->] (D)  to node [above right] {\scriptsize$\pi_2^E$} (C);
				\node at (2,1.5) {$\mathrm{wpb}/\mathrm{psht}$};
			\end{scope}
		\end{tikzpicture}
	\end{center}
	In particular, we have $N = (\iota_1^N)_*; (\iota_2^N)^*$ and $E= (\iota_1^E)_*; (\iota_2^E)^*$. By Lemma \ref{lem:laxdblefunctor}, \[\overline{T}(M, N,R) = (T(\pi_1^M, \iota_1^N, \iota_1^R))_* ; \overline{T}(\mathrm{id}_A, \mathrm{id}_C, \mathrm{id}_X); (T(\pi_2^M, \iota_2^N, \iota_2^R))^*\] so if $\overline{T}$ is normal, we have $\overline{T}(M, N,R) = (T(\pi_1^M, \iota_1^N, \iota_1^R))_* ; (T(\pi_2^M, \iota_2^N, \iota_2^R))^*$. By the universal property of the collage, we obtain that there is a unique function $\mathbf{cl}{(E)} \to T(\mathbf{gr}{(M)},\mathbf{cl}{(N)}, \mathbf{cl}{(E)})$ such that:
		\begin{center}
		\begin{tikzpicture}[thick,xscale=0.4, yscale =0.5, decoration={ markings, mark=at position 0.5 with {\arrow{|}}}]
			\node (A) at (-3,-3) {

				\draw [->, postaction=decorate] (B) -- node [ above] {\scriptsize$E$} (C);

	In particular, we obtain that $\zeta \circ \iota_1^E = T(\pi_1^M, \iota_1^N, \iota_1^R) \circ \alpha$ and $\zeta \circ \iota_2^E = T(\pi_2^M, \iota_2^N, \iota_2^R) \circ \alpha$ as desired. \qedhere
